Good.\n\nPanel diag factor GPU one-CTA kernel:\n```\n__global__ void potrf_small_kernel(double* A, int b, int* flag)\n{\n  int tid=threadIdx.x;\n  for (int j=0;j<b;j++){\n    if (tid==0){\n      double v=A[j*b+j]; // already updated\n      if (v<=0) atomicExch(flag,1); else A[j*b+j]=sqrt(v);\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n    double d = A[j*b+j];\n    if (!(d>0)) d=1.0; // avoid nan\n    for (int i=j+1+tid;i<b;i+=blockDim.x) A[i*b+j] *= 1.0/d; // divide\n    __syncthreads();\n    // update trailing\n    for (int i=j+1+tid;i<b;i+=blockDim.x){\n      double lij=A[i*b+j];\n      for (int k=j+1;k<=i;k++) A[i*b+k] -= lij*A[k*b+j];\n    }\n    __syncthreads();\n  }\n}\n```\nWait race: the update uses A[k*b+j] for k>j, which is the scaled column computed in previous step (done, barrier). Standard Cholesky uses column-major to make A[k][j] contiguous (column). Our matrix is row-major, so column access is bad. Original algorithm is left-looking with dot products of rows (contiguous) — much better cache behavior: for each element (i,j), sum over k of A[i,k]*A[j,k] (both rows contiguous).
